How answers are graded:
How answers are graded

- Each attempt is compared with your answer and marked right or wrong. The grade is on the whole attempt; the final answer shown is picked out automatically as a guide.
- Numbers match when they are the same value written differently: 0.675, 67.5% and 67.5 percentage points all match, and so do different scales (thousand, million, billion), thousands separators, currency symbols and numbers written as words.
- If the question asks for a particular format (a percentage, a number of decimal places, a unit, a rounding or an order), the attempt must meet it. If it asks for none, any equivalent form is accepted.
- For an ordered list, a title, honorific or article added to an entry is accepted if the order is right either with it or without it.
- Only the value the attempt finally commits to counts. Values reached while working, or considered and set aside, do not. An attempt that offers several values without choosing one is wrong, even if one of them is right.

Full answer
-----------

Based on the documents, Kiwibank’s owner (Kiwi Group Capital) announced the $225 million capital injection on 1 August 2023. For Kiwibank (financial year ending 30 June), the half-year that ended immediately before that announcement was the six months to 30 June 2023 (the second half of FY2023).

In that period, Kiwibank’s home lending book grew by $0.6 billion — equivalent to 1.5 times the banking system’s growth rate for the period. (This is referenced in the discussion of Kiwibank’s growth trajectory leading up to the capital injection, noting it had been “steadily growing its home lending share in recent years” with that half-year growth being $0.6b, or 1.5× system growth.)

Based on the documents, in the six-month period ending just before its owner announced the $225 million capital injection (the half-year to 31 December 2022 / prior reporting period), Kiwibank’s home lending book grew by about $0.6 billion — roughly 1.5 times banking system growth for that period.

This is reflected in the submissions discussing Kiwibank’s performance leading up to the capital injection announcement, where it was noted that the bank had been steadily growing its home lending share, with that half seeing lending increase by approximately $0.6bn (1.5x system).

Full answer
-----------

Based on the documents, Kiwibank’s owner (Kiwi Group Capital) announced the $225 million capital injection on 1 August 2023. The financial half-year that ended immediately before that was the six months to 30 June 2023 (the second half of Kiwibank’s 2022/23 financial year).

In that period, Kiwibank’s home lending book grew by about $1.1 billion — roughly 1.5 times system (market) growth — reaching a lending share that had been steadily increasing in recent years (around 7.5% of the home loan market).

So the answer is approximately $1.1 billion (around 1.5× the banking system’s growth rate for that half).

Full answer

To answer this question, I need to identify:

1. When Kiwibank's owner announced the $225 million capital injection
2. Which half of Kiwibank's financial year last ended before this announcement
3. The home lending growth during that specific half-year period

From Document 4 (BNZ submission), footnote 5 states: "(1 August 2023). Kiwibank owner to invest $225m in growing bank's home loan business. Stuff." This confirms the capital injection was announced on August 1, 2023.

From Document 4, footnote 7: "(23 February 2023). Kiwibank half-year results for the six months to 31 December 2022." This shows Kiwibank's financial year ends June 30 (with half-years ending December 31 and June 30).

The half-years ending before August 1, 2023 were:

- First half FY23: ended December 31, 2022
- Second half FY23: ended June 30, 2023

The most recent half-year end before August 1, 2023 was June 30, 2023 (second half of FY23).

Now for the growth figures:

- Document 4 explicitly states: "Kiwibank has announced that 'the bank grew its home lending book by $0.6b (1.5 x the banking system) during the first half [of FY23]'"
- Document 3 states regarding FY23 full-year results: "the CEO noted 'Home lending growth of $1.1 billion, (or 1.5x market)'"

Since FY23 = first half (Jul-Dec 2022) + second half (Jan-Jun 2023): Second half growth = Full year growth - First half growth = $1.1 billion - $0.6 billion = $0.5 billion

Therefore, Kiwibank's home lending book grew by $500 million in the half-year ending June 30, 2023 (the second half of FY23), which was the last half-year to end before the August 1, 2023 capital injection announcement.

$500 million

The last half of Kiwibank's financial year to end before that announcement was the first half of FY23 (six months to 31 December 2022).

> "the bank grew its home lending book by $0.6b (1.5 × the banking system) during the first half [of FY23]."

From Document 3: "the bank grew its home lending book by $0.6b (1.5 x the banking system) during the first half [of FY23], as Kiwibank continued to deepen its relationship with advisers and grow the expertise of its own home loan specialist team." - This is from the Kiwibank half-year results for the six months to 31 December 2022 (FY23 first half).

Kiwibank's Home Lending Growth

Based on the documents provided, Kiwibank's home lending book grew by $1.1 billion (described as "1.5x market" growth) in the period relevant to this question.

As set out in ASB's submission (Document 3):

- Kiwibank announced its FY23 results (for the financial year ended June 2023), which the CEO described as the bank's best in its history, with "Home lending growth of $1.1 billion, (or 1.5x market)."
- Alongside these results, Kiwibank announced a $225 million cash injection from its owner (Kiwi Group Capital), via reinvestment of funds from the sale of its former investment and insurance businesses, to accelerate growth.

The $1.1 billion home lending growth figure is the specific growth reported in the financial period that ended immediately before the capital injection announcement.

Kiwibank's Home Lending Growth

Based on the documents, Kiwibank's owner (Kiwi Group Capital, backed by the New Zealand Government) announced the $225 million capital injection around August 2023, as described as "recent" in ASB's September 2023 submission.

Kiwibank's financial year ends 30 June. The half of its financial year that last ended before the capital injection announcement is therefore the second half of FY23 (January–June 2023), which closed on 30 June 2023.

According to ASB's submission (section 9.15), Kiwibank reported:

> "Home lending growth of $1.1 billion, (or 1.5x market)"

— announced as part of its FY23 results (the year ending 30 June 2023).

Therefore, Kiwibank's home lending book grew by $1.1 billion in the period referenced.

According to BNZ's submission (Document 4), Kiwibank grew its home lending book by $0.6 billion (1.5x the banking system growth rate) during the first half of FY23. This was announced in Kiwibank's half-year results (for the six months to 31 December 2022). The $225 million capital injection from Kiwi Group Capital was subsequently announced on 1 August 2023.

Reasoning
---------

Full answer
-----------

The ANZ “Simple Switch” pilot ran in April 2023 (Document 2, para ~123).

Kiwibank’s financial year ends in June, so April 2023 falls in Kiwibank’s second half (Jan–Jun 2023).

From Document 3:

- Kiwibank’s full FY23 home lending grew by $1.1 billion (para near 9.15 / footnote 39).
- In the first half of FY23 (six months to Dec 2022) its home lending book grew by $0.6 billion (para near 9.16 / footnote 7).

So for the second half (when the pilot occurred):

$1.1 bn − $0.6 bn = $0.5 bn

Answer: Kiwibank’s home lending book grew by $0.5 billion (about $500 million) in that half.

Full answer
-----------

Finding the Answer

Step 1: When did ANZ pilot Simple Switch?

From ANZ's submission (Document 2): "In April 2023 we launched a pilot, Simple Switch, to gauge whether we could offer a streamlined switching process under the CCCFA exception."

Step 2: Which half of Kiwibank's financial year does April 2023 fall in?

Kiwibank's FY23 runs July 2022 – June 2023 (confirmed by BNZ referencing "Kiwibank half-year results for the six months to 31 December 2022" as the first half of FY23). April 2023 falls in the second half (January–June 2023).

Step 3: What was Kiwibank's home lending book growth?

Two data points from the documents:

- First half of FY23 (Jul–Dec 2022): BNZ's submission quotes Kiwibank's half-year results: "the bank grew its home lending book by $0.6b* (1.5 x the banking system) during the first half [of FY23]"*
- Full year FY23 (Jul 2022–Jun 2023): ASB's submission quotes Kiwibank's FY23 full-year results: "Home lending growth of $1.1 billion, (or 1.5x market)"

Second half growth = $1.1b − $0.6b = $0.5 billion

Kiwibank's home lending book grew by approximately $0.5 billion in the half of its financial year when ANZ piloted Simple Switch (the second half of FY23).

Answer

ANZ launched its Simple Switch pilot in April 2023 (Document 2, ¶123). This falls within Kiwibank's FY23 second half (January–June 2023), as Kiwibank's financial year runs from July to June.

The documents report Kiwibank's home lending growth for the first half of FY23 (six months to 31 December 2022): Kiwibank announced it "grew its home lending book by $0.6 billion (1.5× the banking system)" (Document 3, ¶2.6 and footnote 7).

No growth figure is provided in the documents for Kiwibank's second half of FY23 (which contains April 2023). The $0.6 billion figure is the closest available data, reported for the most recently disclosed half-year period at the time of the Simple Switch pilot.

Reasoning
---------

Let me find the relevant information from the documents.

First, I need to find when ANZ piloted Simple Switch. From Document 2 (ANZ submission), paragraph 123: "In April 2023 we launched a pilot, Simple Switch, to gauge whether we could offer a streamlined switching process under the CCCFA exception for customers looking to refinance their existing lending."

So the pilot was launched in April 2023.

Full answer
-----------

Kiwibank's Home Lending Book Growth

Based on the documents, Kiwibank grew its home lending book by $0.6 billion (1.5× the banking system growth rate) during the first half of its FY23 financial year (six months to 31 December 2022), as reported in Kiwibank's half-year results (cited in BNZ's submission, paragraph 2.6(c)).

ANZ piloted its "Simple Switch" process in April 2023 (ANZ submission, paragraph 123). Given Kiwibank's fiscal year runs July–June, this falls within the second half of FY23 (January–June 2023). The documents do not provide a specific home lending growth figure for that particular half-year; the $0.6 billion figure cited is the only Kiwibank home lending book growth number available in the submissions.
